        Research on the UFO mov��nr has been by no means ex�e�sive, but

it has been quite varied.               Hisrorical (Jacobs, 1975), bibliographical

1968; 1974) and more general              studies (Sag�• and Page, 1972;               P.e-riry,

1980;     Story, 1980 ) , ha'� been made, but only social sci ence research
wHl be exai:lined here.             Studies within this area can be roughly

divided into three main !:>ubject areas :            Firstly, those conce!'ltrating
mainly on the question of why people see UFOs .                            Secondly, those which

\f.<y do people see UFOs?

        The earliest �xplanations of why people see UFOs are probably
those expressed by the US Deparonenc of Defense,                            in a press release in
1949, ��ere UFO reports were said to be the result of either mis­
interpretations of various convention2l objects , a mild form of mass
hysteria , or hoaxes (Hynek, 1978).

        Tne ex:planation that most UFO sightings are due to errors of the
human mi nd and senses is widely accepted by ' sceptics' and 'believers '
alike.     The problem with this explanation is tha� i t is limited.                         lt
cannot account for why UFOs are consistently misinterpreted in a parti cular
way (i.e. as extra-terrestrial vehicles) or why celestial objects such
as the planet. Verru.s should suddenly becane a source of conf-<.l.Sio.; to

people .

        The hoax explanation can only account for a small number of
Lcidents (less than 2% of reports analysea by Project Blue Book ) and in
 ;
